# CLAUDE.md

This codebase is a comprehensive formalization of category theory in Coq/Rocq. It contains 563 proof files (the `_CoqProject` build set — 566 `.v` files exist on disk, the difference being three unbuilt probes under `doc/plan/books/`) with over 170,000 lines implementing core categorical concepts with zero axioms in the core theory — spanning the classical canon from categories, functors, and adjunctions through monadicity, the adjoint functor theorems, enriched and higher structures (bicategories, pseudo double categories), Lawvere theories, multicategories and operads, and elementary topos theory.

"Zero axioms in the core theory" is narrower than it sounds, and docs/AXIOMS.md is the authority for exactly how narrow. Two separate facts hold. First, the only `Axiom`/`Parameter` declarations anywhere in the library are the three `Phase` parameters of the ZX-calculus instance, all in `Instance/ZX.v`. Second, `Print Assumptions` reports "Closed under the global context" for an ENUMERATED list of headline definitions, audited by `make print-assumptions`. That second fact is *not* a claim about every definition in the library — docs/AXIOMS.md says so in terms — and no directory-wide certification of `Theory/`, `Structure/`, or `Construction/` has been run. The concrete instance layers (`Instance/`, `Theory/Coq/`) do use stdlib axioms, enumerated there. Separately, many of the advanced flagship theorems are proven *parametrically* — as axiom-free conditionals over an abstract structure — and only some are instantiated by a concrete in-tree model; docs/INHABITATION.md records which.

## Commands

### Building the Library

```bash
# Build the entire library (default: Rocq 9.1)
make

# Build for specific version (if using Nix)
nix-shell -p coqPackages_9_1.coq --run make

# Build using Nix flake (the default package is category-theory_9_1)
nix build
# ...or select a version explicitly, e.g. nix build .#category-theory_8_20

# Clean build artifacts
make clean
make fullclean  # removes Makefile.coq as well

# Install library
make install

# Check for admitted proofs or TODOs
make todo
```

### Single File Development

```bash
# Compile a single file
coqc -R . Category Theory/Category.v

# Interactive development (in coqide/vscoq)
# Ensure _CoqProject is loaded with: -R . Category
```

### Proof Development Patterns

The library uses custom tactics in `Lib/Tactics.v`:
- `cat` - standard simplification for category proofs
- `cat_simpl` - more aggressive simplification with program obligations
- `proper` - for proving morphism respectfulness
- `equivalence` - for proving equivalence relations

## Architecture

### Core Abstraction Hierarchy

The library implements category theory through **setoid-based morphisms** where morphisms form equivalence classes rather than using strict equality. This fundamental design choice cascades through the entire architecture:

```
Category (homsets are setoids with ≈ equivalence)
    ↓
Functor (preserves equivalence, not just equality)
    ↓
Natural Transformation (naturality respects ≈)
    ↓
Adjunction/Monad/Kan Extension
```

### Universe Polymorphism Strategy

The library uses three universe parameters `{o h p}` throughout:
- `o` - objects universe level
- `h` - hom-sets universe level
- `p` - proof/proposition universe level
- Constraint: `h <= p` ensures morphism proofs can reference morphisms

This allows categories of categories at any level without universe inconsistencies.

### Duality Architecture

**Key insight**: Duality is built into definitions so `C^op^op = C` by reflexivity.

This requires symmetric laws in core definitions:
- `comp_assoc` AND `comp_assoc_sym` in Category
- `naturality` AND `naturality_sym` in Natural transformations

Benefits:
- Comonads are one line: `Definition Comonad := @Monad (C^op) (M^op)`
- Dual proofs are often automatic
- No code duplication for dual concepts

### Proof Obligation Management

The library uses Coq's Program mechanism extensively:
1. Definitions use `Program Instance` to defer proof obligations
2. `Obligation Tactic := cat_simpl` automates most proofs
3. Remaining obligations proven with specific tactics
4. This separates mathematical content from proof details

### Instance Resolution Strategy

Categories are discovered through:
1. Type classes for concepts (Category, Functor, etc.)
2. `Existing Instance` for conditional instances
3. Notation-driven inference with category hints
4. Explicit category parameters when inference fails

Example: `f ∘[C] g` specifies category C when needed.

## Key Files and Concepts

### Theory Core
- **Theory/Category.v**: Defines categories with setoid hom-sets
- **Theory/Functor.v**: Functors preserve equivalence — with the §I.3/§I.5 closure and reflection results: `Full_Compose`/`Faithful_Compose`, and `faithful_reflects_monic`/`_epic` (a faithful functor reflects both cancellation properties), from which the concrete-category corollary in Theory/Concrete/Morphisms.v follows. Subcategory inclusions are faithful generically (`Incl_Faithful`, Construction/Subcategory.v); the converse "full inclusion ⟹ full subcategory" needs the selected morphisms to be closed under `≈`, which the `Subcategory` record does not supply and which Construction/Subcategory/FullConverse.v proves NECESSARY by counterexample
- **Theory/Shapes.v**: functors out of the walking shapes (Mac Lane §I.3) — objects ≃ functors `_1 ⟶ C` (`Point`/`point_of`), arrows ≃ functors `_2 ⟶ C` (`Walk`/`arrow_of`), composable pairs ≃ functors `_3 ⟶ C` (`Functor_of_Pair`), each an explicit construction pair with both round trips rather than a bijection class, the `_1` and `_2` ones landing at the strict `Functor_StrictEq_Setoid`. The category-level comparisons `One_Fun_iso : [_1, C] ≅[Cat] C` and `Two_Fun_Arrow : [_2, C] ≅[Cat] Arrow C` — both at `C : Category@{o Set Set}`, forced on the `_2` side because `Fun` identifies source and target hom universes while `_2`'s homs live in `Set` — close the gap `Construction/Arrow.v` previously disclosed as documentation-level — read their strength precisely: `≅[Cat]` is EQUIVALENCE, `Arrow_Fun_Arrow_strict` is strict on one side only, and the `StrictCat` isomorphism is explicitly not delivered (the object component would be a Leibniz equality of functor records, which `fmap_id` alone does not give). Seven Sketches instantiations: the three functors `_2 ⟶ _3` of Example 3.36 exhibited as named morphisms and proved pairwise non-equivalent, the six-functor count of Exercise 3.37 DERIVED by transporting the morphism count of `3` rather than enumerated, and Exercise 3.45's "functors from 1 to Sets are sets" turned from comment prose into a theorem. Note the name: the pre-existing `Instance/Shapes.v` is unrelated (its `Shape` indexes tries)
- **Theory/Diagram.v**: Diagrams and commutativity — a diagram in `C` of shape `G` is a quiver homomorphism `Diagram G C := QuiverHomomorphism G (QuiverOfCat C)`, path denotation `dpath` composes edge labels along a `tlist` path, and `Commutative D` quantifies over EVERY pair of vertices and EVERY pair of parallel paths of arbitrary length (Mac Lane §I.1, no shape privileged — unlike the tree's figure-fixing statements `Construction/Sq.v:50`'s `dsq`, `Structure/Cone.v:30`'s `cone_coherence`, and `Theory/Morphisms/Stability.v:55`'s `is_pullback_commutes`, the last two of which are recovered as instances). The bridge to the free category runs both ways with both round trips proved — `FunctorOfDiagram`/`DiagramOfFunctor`, `functor_of_diagram_of_functor` in StrictCat's hom-setoid and `diagram_of_functor_of_diagram` in `QuiverCategory` — and under it `Commutative` coincides with the Seven Sketches diagram-as-functor predicate `CommutativeShape` (`commutative_iff_shape`, `shape_iff_commutative`); respectfulness and naturality of the two maps are disclosed as NOT established, so the two maps are mutually inverse up to `≈` pointwise — weaker than a bijection of the underlying types (the round trips are up to `≈`, not `=`) and weaker than one of the setoid quotients (without respectfulness the maps do not descend), and in particular not a hom-setoid isomorphism. Walking square and triangle with the general predicate proved EQUIVALENT to the single two-composite equation (`square_commutative_iff`, `triangle_commutative_iff`), the naturality square as such a diagram (`naturality_square_commutative_iff`), Riehl's Lemma 1.6.20 for arbitrary path length (`faithful_reflects_commutative`), and the Solver-style term denotation `dterm` over `Construction/Free/Quiver.v`'s `Mor` with `dterm_dpath`/`commutative_dterm`. Concrete axiom-free witnesses in `Coq` escape BOTH vacuity traps, each escape proved: the shape genuinely has two distinct parallel paths (`sq_via_B_neq_via_C`, in Theory/Diagram.v) and the target is provably not thin (`coq_parallel_not_equiv`), so `coq_square_commutative` is not commuting by collapse — and `coq_square_not_commutative` is a provably NON-commuting diagram over the SAME quiver into the SAME category (Theory/Diagram/Examples.v). The empty path is not inert: `loop_commutative_iff` proves that over the walking loop `Commutative` is EXACTLY "the drawn endomorphism is `id`", with concrete `Coq` witnesses both ways (`coq_loop_negb_not_commutative`, `coq_loop_id_commutative`); the header is careful about what this separates — `dsq` quantifies over arbitrary OBJECTS, so `dsq X X X X e id id id` reduces to `e ≈ id` and CAN state the loop condition, the real distinction being that `Commutative` FORCES it from the shape alone (the empty path is always available as a competitor) while `dsq` must be instantiated to it, and `cone_coherence`/`is_pullback_commutes` are record FIELDS that cannot be freely instantiated at all. The two walking shapes are acyclic, proved by a rank argument (`sq_endo_paths_are_nil`, `tri_endo_paths_are_nil`) rather than inferred from empty self-edge sets — which would not suffice, a directed cycle of length ≥ 2 having no self-edge. The Seven Sketches footnote packaging (commutes ⟺ factors through the preorder reflection) IS proved, in both directions (`commutative_shape_factors`, `factors_commutative_shape`, `factors_commutative_shape'`, `commutative_factors`), and packaged as the single biconditional `commutes_iff_factors`, with the forward factorization an equality of FUNCTORS in StrictCat's hom-setoid (`shape_factor_functor`), over **Construction/PreorderReflection.v** — the preorder reflection as the quotient of a category by the TOTAL hom-congruence, `PreorderReflect C := Quotient C (TotalRel C)` with `preorder_reflect_thin` and the identity-on-objects `Reflect`, plus the universal property `ThinLift`/`ThinLift_proj`/`ThinLift_unique` showing every functor into a thin category factors through `Reflect` uniquely — so it is THE universal thin quotient, which is what earns the word "reflection" rather than an nLab pointer. This is NOT the nLab's *posetal* reflection, which identifies OBJECTS by mutual relatedness; `preorder_reflect_obj` proves objects are untouched definitionally. It needed no new machinery (`Construction/Quotient.v` already has the hom-congruence quotient and `QuotientLift`; all FOUR `HomCongruence` fields are immediate — reflexivity is the derived `cong_refl`, not a field), and it supplies work item 1 of issue #803's SEVEN, the other six remaining open; #803's plan supplies the module name and `PreorderReflect`, the other names being this file's own
- **Theory/Natural/Transformation.v**: Natural transformations — the arrows-only (object-free) presentation is Theory/Natural/Transformation/Arrows.v (Mac Lane §I.4 exercise 5): `ArrowTransform` indexes a morphism `S c ~> T c'` by each arrow `f : c ~> c'` under two splice laws, with the round trip to `Transform` (components `τ_arr id`) both ways, the four uniqueness statements, and the correspondence packaged as an `Isomorphism` in `Sets` between the two setoids. Two results worth knowing: respectfulness of the family in its arrow argument is DERIVABLE from the two laws rather than extra data (each law supplies one leg of the detour through `f ∘ id`), and the two splice laws are genuinely INDEPENDENT — `dom_family`/`cod_family`, indexed by the WALKING ARROW `_2` with the parallel-pair category as target (the target is what supplies the two non-equivalent arrows that separate; the index is where the domain and codomain of a non-identity arrow come apart), satisfy one law and refute the other, so neither field can be dropped. A closing section settles the companion question with countermodels over a hom-setoid that genuinely quotients: NEITHER law alone implies respectfulness (`left_law_alone_gives_no_respect`, `right_law_alone_gives_no_respect`), so the derivation's use of both laws is essential rather than an artifact of the proof. This is unlike `naturality`/`naturality_sym` in the parent file, which are one equation in two orientations
- **Theory/Adjunction.v**: The most important concept - adjunctions
- **Adjunction/Conjugate.v**: Mac Lane §IV.7 — conjugate natural transformations, the mate OPERATOR upgraded to the mate RELATION. `Conjugate A A' σ τ` IS the hom-set square `⌊k ∘ σ x⌋' ≈ τ a ∘ ⌊k⌋` quantified over every transposable `k`, not its value at one distinguished argument, with the inverse-transpose reading `ConjugateFrom` proved equivalent (`conjugate_iff_from`); `conjugate_characterizations` proves four pasting forms all equivalent to it — the two mate formulas `τ = U'ε ∘ U'σU ∘ η'U` and `σ = ε'F ∘ F'τF ∘ F'η`, which are what `mate`/`mate_inv` compute, and the unit and counit equations `τF ∘ η = U'σ ∘ η'` and `ε ∘ σU = ε' ∘ F'τ`, the counit equation having had no in-tree statement at all and the unit equation only the degenerate `F = F'` form of Theory/Bicategory/Adjunction.v's `mate_charac`/`mate_unit_compat`. Each transformation of left adjoints has one and only one conjugate and dually (`conjugate_unique_right`/`conjugate_unique_left`, by evaluating the square at the universal arrows ε and id), packaged as `conjugate_bijection : (F' ⟹ F) ≊ (U ⟹ U')` in Sets — the ordinary-category form of Theory/Bicategory/Mates.v's `mate_iso`, developed over arbitrary C and D in a seven-module cone with no bicategorical machinery (the Cat route stays available at any size, at the cost of Instance/Cat and the bicategory chain). Riehl §4.3(iv) rides it: `conjugate_invertible_iff` (σ pointwise invertible ⟺ its conjugate is) is proved from the square alone, using neither transformation's naturality, so Mates.v's descope ledger 10 is untouched — `conjugate_id`/`conjugate_compose` are that ledger entry's identity-bounding-cell shadow, not its discharge. The bridge is Instance/Cat/Bicategory/Conjugate.v: padding transforms (`F' ◯ Id ⟹ Id ◯ F` is not `F' ⟹ F` definitionally, the two functor records carrying different `fmap_respects`/`fmap_id`/`fmap_comp` fields, which are data here and which record eta cannot identify), `Cat_conj_mate_agrees`/`Cat_conj_mate_inv_agrees` identifying `conj_mate`/`conj_mate_inv` with `mate`/`mate_inv` at identity bounding cells through the transparent `Cat_Adjunction_BicatAdjunction` rather than the Qed-opaque `Cat_BicatAdjunction_Adjunction_iff`, `conjugate_bijection_is_mate_iso`, equating the forward leg of `conjugate_bijection` with `mate_iso`'s forward leg conjugated by the two padding isomorphisms, the `Cat_mate_inv_unfold_raw` the tree lacked, and the boundary witness `mate_of_iso_not_invertible` — an invertible 2-cell whose mate over the non-identity bounding functor `Erase _2` is not invertible
- **Theory/Monad.v**: Monads as endofunctors with structure
- **Theory/EckmannHilton.v**: The abstract Eckmann–Hilton argument (Mac Lane §II.5 Ex 5; Eckmann–Hilton 1962) over a BARE setoid — imports `Category.Lib` only, the principle being pre-categorical: two `Proper` binary operations with SEPARATE two-sided units satisfying the interchange law have coinciding units (`eh_units` — the strictly-more-general piece, since the in-tree instance never needed it), coinciding operations (`eh_ops`), and the single operation commutative and associative (`eh_comm`/`eh_assoc`, with `eh_g_comm`/`eh_g_assoc` on the other side), bundled as the pinned `eckmann_hilton` (a right-associated `∧`-prod — `≈` is Type-valued, so no `and`); all hypotheses discharge as explicit positional arguments (`Local Set Default Proof Using "All"`, the Theory/Category/Monoid.v:919 precedent, since they appear only in proofs). Structure/Semiadditive.v's convolution trio `conv_conv_pr`/`conv_comm`/`conv_assoc` is REFACTORED to route through it with statements byte-identical — `conv_interchange` and the four `conv_*_zero_*` lemmas survive unchanged as the instantiation's inputs, both units being `zero_mor`. Other in-tree instances of the principle, cited in the header: Instance/Cat/TwoCategory.v's `NatBase_centre` (eq_refl form — 2-cells on an identity 1-cell in the strict 2-category Cat, Mac Lane §II.5 Ex 8's centre) and the essay mentions in Structure/Monoid.v:90 and Structure/Group.v:73
- **Theory/Centre.v**: The centre of a category (Mac Lane §II.5 Ex 8; Riehl §1.7 Ex 1.7.iv, her exercise being this verbatim) — `centre_monoid := hom_monoid ([C, C]) Id[C]`, the natural endotransformations of the identity functor as a `MonObject` by PURE Deloop.v reuse (zero new monoid obligations; four eq_refl Examples pin carrier/unit/op), with **`centre_commutative`** proved from a SINGLE naturality square: `fmap[Id]` is definitionally the identity, so α's naturality at the morphism `transform[β] x` IS the commutation `α_x ∘ β_x ≈ β_x ∘ α_x` (`centre_commutes_at`, two tactics; only α's naturality is consumed). The Eckmann–Hilton route (`centre_commutative_EH` via Theory/EckmannHilton.v's `eh_comm` — this file is that theorem's third consumer) is delivered as a disclosed CONSISTENCY EXHIBIT, not an independent derivation, with the honesty MACHINE-CHECKED rather than asserted: `centre_hcompose_is_vcomp` shows the Godement composite has the same components as vertical composition at Id by `eq_refl` (so the "two operations" are one operation and its argument swap — stated componentwise deliberately, `nat_hcompose` landing at `Id ◯ Id` being a transport question the header declines to paper over with a unitor), and `centre_interchange_forces_commutative` derives commutativity from the interchange SHAPE alone by instantiating at identities (so interchange is not a weaker input). Computations: `centre_Sets_trivial`(`_nat`/`_subsingleton`) — the centre of Sets is trivial, by probing naturality at the constant map from the singleton setoid, constructive, universe-clean after fixing a found Set-pinning (`@{o so}` binders on the probe); `centre_Coq_trivial` likewise (`=` there, elements of Coq not morphisms). Header carries the three-way DISTINCTION with kinds — this centre (commutative MONOID) vs the premonoidal centre (`Centre`, Structure/Binoidal/Central.v:256 — a CATEGORY of central morphisms, which is why that name was left untaken here) vs the Drinfeld centre (Structure/Monoidal/Drinfeld.v — a BRAIDED MONOIDAL CATEGORY) — and the Grp/Ab identifications as explicitly-labelled remarks (fg-Ab = (ℤ,·) needs a torsion argument the tree lacks). 35/35 constants closed under the global context
- **Monad/Strong.v**: Strong monads — a monad whose functor carries a tensorial strength compatible with η and μ (Kock/Moggi)
- **Comonad/Core.v**: Covariant comonad API over the one-line `Comonad := @Monad (C^op) (M^op)` — extract/duplicate/extend as definitional op-reads; co-Kleisli category (Comonad/CoKleisli.v), co-Eilenberg-Moore coalgebras (Comonad/Coalgebra.v), costrength (Comonad/Strong.v), and the adjunction-induced comonad with co-resolutions by duality (Comonad/Duality.v); Env/Store/Traced instances in Instance/Coq/Comonad/. Monad resolutions: the Kleisli and Eilenberg-Moore free/forgetful adjunctions in Monad/Kleisli/Adjunction.v and Monad/Eilenberg/Moore/Adjunction.v
- **Monad/Monadicity/Beck.v**: Monadicity — the Eilenberg-Moore comparison functor with both triangle theorems and the `Monadic` predicate (Monad/Comparison.v, over a transparent `Adjunction_Induced_Monad`); split coequalizers with absoluteness and reflexive pairs (Structure/Coequalizer/{Split,Reflexive}.v); the Beck engine room — canonical split forks, `EM_Forget` reflects isos and creates U-split coequalizers (Monad/Monadicity/BeckObjects.v); crude monadicity fully proven (Monad/Monadicity/Crude.v); Beck's precise theorem with conservativity derived from creation and the `monadic_creates` converse at `EM_Forget` (Monad/Monadicity/Beck.v); the Dubuc adjoint-triangle lifting theorem (Monad/Lifting.v); identity-monad witness (Monad/Monadicity/Examples.v)
- **Theory/Algebra/Rig.v**: Rigs (semirings) — Seven Sketches §5.3.1 Definition 5.36 clause for clause (multiplication deliberately NOT commutative) over a setoid carrier, following Instance/CMon.v's naming: `RigObject`/`RigHom`/the category `Rig`, with forgetful functors to `CMon` (additive half) and to `Mon Sets` (multiplicative half as an internal monoid in (Sets, ∏)). The one-object reading is a theorem both ways: `DeloopRig` equips the delooping of the multiplicative monoid with a `Preadditive` instance (the four rig clauses ARE the Preadditive fields on one object), `EndRig` reads a rig off the endomorphism hom of any object of any preadditive category, and the rig-side round trip is definitional on all four data fields, packaged as the identity-carrier iso `EndRig_DeloopRig` (full record equality is blocked only by proof-carrying law fields; the category-side composite is deferred exactly as Construction/Deloop.v defers its §I.3 dictionary) — all packaged as `rig_iff_one_object_preadditive`. Instances: `Nat_Rig` (Example 5.37), `Bool_Rig` (Example 5.38, (false, ∨, true, ∧)); `RingObject` = rig + additive inverses (Example 5.42, mirroring Additive-over-Preadditive) with negation-preservation a THEOREM (`RigHom_neg`, by uniqueness of inverses — ring homs are just rig homs) and the axiom-free witness `Int_Ring` over stdlib Z (chosen over the suggested ℝ to keep the instance layer axiom-clean). ℕ is initial (`Rig_Initial`, Seven Sketches Ex 6.7: the unique hom is the n-fold sum of 1). Theory/Algebra/Rig/Connections.v records, by `eq_refl`, that Monad/Graded.v's `AndGrade` is the multiplicative half of `Bool_Rig` and that `Nat_Rig`'s operations are the object actions of skeletal FinSet's coproduct/product — the decategorified shadow of Example 5.37
- **Theory/Metacategory/General.v**: Arrows-only metacategories over an arbitrary arrow type — Mac Lane §§I.1/I.8's presentation with axiom (iii) stated as the CONJUNCTION Mac Lane wrote (both sibling files encode it as an implication and say so in `jww` notes; their form is vacuously true). Identity uniqueness (`mident_unique_src`/`_tgt`), and the separation result `weak_identity_not_unique`: a four-arrow countermodel refuting identity uniqueness under axiom (i)'s weak one-sided form — the axiom-(iii) conjunction is carried identically by both records, so the separation is about axiom (i), and the guarded conjunctive presentation as a whole is a correction, not a restyling. Both passages: `Category_from_Metacategory` (unconditional) and `ToArrows : ∀ C, ObjUIP-style hypothesis → Metacategory` (bundling homs into one arrow sort transports along object equalities, the campaign's recurring honest hypothesis), with the comparison `ToArrows_Functor` proved Faithful + Full + EssentiallySurjective. `id_unique`/`id_unique_left`/`id_unique_right` land at `Category` level (Theory/Category.v, Riehl §1.1). The sibling files' headers are repaired to state what they actually prove — including the machine-checked fact that their unguarded `identity` field leaves every example metacategory objectless (`Three` is the empty category) and that the naive `->`→`/\` fix would make their records uninhabitable — in ArrowsOnly.v the arrow type must first be cut to the table's support, while in Metacategory.v even that is insufficient (its unguarded `identity` field admits no identities at all, support-restricted or not) and the guards must be restored first; the `jww` TODOs stay, with these diagnoses. `MetaRoundTrip`/`MetaRoundTrip_Equivalence` form the metacategory-side composite under the analogous UIP hypothesis; no metacategory-level equivalence notion is defined, so no comparison with `M` is stated at that level. ETAC, the elementary first-order language itself, is deep-embedded in Theory/Metacategory/ETAC.v (Mac Lane §§II.1-II.2 + Awodey §3.1): two-sorted de Bruijn syntax over dom/cod/identity/composite/equality atoms, Type-valued satisfaction `holds` over arrow BUNDLES `Arr C` (endpoint comparisons Leibniz on objects — the object sort's own relation, disclosed; hom-setoid `≈` exactly at the arrow equations, cast along sigma-packaged endpoint equalities with the `acast` kit), the syntactic dual with `dual_invol`, `closed_below`/`sentence` with environment-independence of sentences, the semantic duality lemma `holds_dual` (C ⊨ Σ* iff C^op ⊨ Σ under the `op_arr` environment transport — the op-transport of each arrow-equation satisfaction proved on literal-pair bundles reached by the propositional `arr_eta`, where every destruct is variable-to-variable), and `duality_principle : valid p → valid (dual p)` (instantiate at C^op; no double-op unwinding). The functor extension (remark 2) is scoped to the bundle-level `arr_fmap_op` commutation with the deferral disclosed (validity is not isomorphism-invariant — object atoms are strict, stated in the header). The §IV.3 dualization-table rows land beside it: the inverse-pair quartet (`op_Retraction_of_Section` etc., Theory/Morphisms/Duality.v) and `Full_op`/`Faithful_op` with converses for an ARBITRARY functor (Functor/Opposite.v — previously reachable only through the equivalence detour)
- **Theory/Category/Monoid.v**: Mac Lane's §I.2 remark that the set-based definition of a category IS a monoid for the composable-pairs product over its object set — the germ of internal categories, formalized at the set level (the general internal-category theory over pullbacks is explicitly out of scope). Two-sorted graphs `Graph` (objects strict, arrows a setoid) with the fibred product `Composable` and its UMP; `SpanMonoid G` packages unit + multiplication with boundary and monoid laws up to `≈`. Direction one is unconditional: `Category_from_SpanMonoid : SpanMonoid G → Category`. Direction two, `Category_SpanMonoid : ∀ C, HomRigid C → SpanMonoid (Category_Graph C)`, carries an explicit `HomRigid` hypothesis (transport of a morphism along object-equality proofs is proof-independent; implied by `ObjUIP`, axiom-free via Hedberg when object equality is decidable) — the obstruction is real (consumed by `arrow_mul_respects` and `arrow_mul_assoc`: two proofs of one composability equation differ by a loop at the *intermediate* object, which the outer setoid freedom cannot repair) and provably unavoidable: `arrow_mul_respects_forces_UIP` shows discharging it uniformly entails UIP for every type, via an `option A` countermodel whose `None` has provably contractible loops by encode-decode. In Mac Lane's set-based reading the question does not arise. Round trips: objects definitional both ways by `eq_refl`, and the Category-side comparison assembles into `Category_monoid_iso : C ≅[Cat] Category_from_SpanMonoid (Category_SpanMonoid C rigid)` — in this library the same strength as an equivalence of categories (Cat's hom-setoid is `Functor_Setoid`), and the strongest packaging available, with the genuinely extra content in the construction: both functors identity-on-objects, one hom leg definitional
- **Theory/OGraph.v**: Mac Lane §II.7 display (3) — categories ARE monoids in O-graphs, with the monoidal structure built in FULL rather than approximated. Over a fixed node type O: `OGraph O` (an edge family with per-pair setoids), identity-on-nodes morphisms (`OGraphHom` carries NO node action — that is what "identity-on-objects" means here), the category `OGrph O`, the trivial O-graph `OGraph_unit` (edges `x = y`), and the composable-pairs product `OGraph_prod` over a primitive-projection record `Composable` (cmid/cleft/cright). **`OGrph_Monoidal` is a real `Monoidal (OGrph O)`** — genuine tensor BIFUNCTOR, both unitors and the associator as isomorphisms, all six naturality fields, and BOTH `ograph_triangle` and `ograph_pentagon` proved, nothing descoped — so **`category_is_monoid_in_OGrph`** is stated against Theory/Algebra/Monoid.v's own `Monoid` class (`OMonoid A := @Monoid (OGrph O) (OGrph_Monoidal O) A`) rather than a bespoke lookalike, and Mac Lane's display (3) IS `mu_assoc`/`mu_unit_left`/`mu_unit_right`. **NO UIP** — not as an axiom, not as a hypothesis: composable pairs are identified by a middle-node equality plus transported `≈`, and every consumer ELIMINATES that equality rather than comparing two proofs of it. The escape is localized to a SETOID CHOICE, and localized as a THEOREM: the trivial O-graph compares its edge-proofs by LEIBNIZ equality, so the left unitor's respectfulness gets `p = p'` handed to it, and `coarse_respectfulness_entails_UIP` (audit-adopted, axiom-free) shows that over the coarsened setoid — all proofs identified, which looks harmless — respectfulness of that very map ENTAILS `NodeUIP O`, not merely resembles it. Correspondingly `I_O` may carry MORE than one loop at a node when O lacks UIP and is still a unit — the product's setoid quotients by exactly that freedom — and "exactly one arrow per node" is NOT claimed. Round trips measured strict-first: the O-graph and the multiplication's underlying map return by `eq_refl` (primitive-projection eta on `Composable` is what buys the second), the unit only pointwise (`eq` has no eta rule, stated as the reason); on the category side obj/hom/homset/id/compose are five separate `eq_refl` Examples, packaged as `ocat_roundtrip_iso : C ≅[Cat] CategoryOfOMonoid (MonoidOfCat C)` with both functors identity on objects AND morphisms — Leibniz equality of the two `Category` records is NOT claimed (law fields are rebuilt), the boundary Rig.v's `EndRig_DeloopRig` also reports. Orientation pinned by computation, not prose: `ocat_mu_is_compose`/`ocat_eta_is_id` are `eq_refl`, so the multiplication IS this library's `compose` (the other orientation would give the opposite category). Witness at `O := poly_unit` recovering Construction/Deloop.v's `Deloop M` fieldwise by `eq_refl`, with `ograph_nat_compose_2_3` computing 2·3 = 5 in (ℕ,+). Universes measured per constant with causes attributed: `OGraph_prod` carries exactly `o <= h`, which is the construction's honest content (a composable pair literally contains a node inside an edge); `OGrph`'s hom=proof identification is `Structure/Monoidal.v`'s own class shape, NOT a restriction on which categories can be read as graphs (`OGraphOfCat` takes an arbitrary `Category@{o h p}`); the definitions carry the Quiver.v/Category.v explicit-binder discipline (the development reports having needed it against minimization; the counterfactual is not machine-checkable and is not claimed as measured). The packaged `category_is_monoid_in_OGrph` is DEGENERATE in its universes — its constraint clause collapses each half to one level, because the round-trip Examples do not carry the binders the definitions do — so the general result lives in the components `MonoidOfCat`/`CategoryOfOMonoid` (only `o <= h`, `h <= p`), a known gap recorded in-file rather than papered over. The Quiver bridge `OGrph_Quiver` is faithful under an EXPLICIT `NodeUIP` hypothesis (the quiver hom-equivalence compares up to an arbitrary family of node self-equalities), with the unconditional question left open rather than asserted. Contrast recorded in-header with Theory/Category/Monoid.v's §I.2 germ, which DOES need `HomRigid`: there a graph is two-sorted so reading a category bundles all homs into one sort and transports along object equalities; here edges stay INDEXED by endpoints, nothing is bundled, and no rigidity is taken. Ex 7's Set half is delivered CONSTRUCTIVELY over FinSet and REFUTED as a blanket principle over `Sets`. `finset_regular` (Instance/FinSet/Regular.v) builds the pseudoinverse by an executable finite search over `Fin.t` (`fin_preimage`, reducing by `eq_refl` on closed input); its inhabited-domain hypothesis is SHARP — `finset_empty_to_one_not_regular` refutes regularity for the unique arrow 0 → 1 — yet costs nothing for splitting, since an epi out of the empty object forces an empty codomain and so `finset_every_epi_splits` holds for EVERY arrow of FinSet with no hypothesis at all. Over `Sets` the blanket statement is not a choice principle but a strictly stronger one, and Instance/Sets/Regular.v proves it axiom-free: `blanket_regularity_entails_LEM` derives `∀ P : Prop, P + (P → False)` from "every arrow with inhabited domain is regular", and `blanket_splitting_entails_LEM` derives the same from the weaker internal choice principle "every epi with inhabited domain splits", by a Diaconescu-shaped countermodel (bool coarsened by P) for which regularity is EXACTLY decidability (`sets_coarsen_regular_iff_dec`) and non-regularity is refutable outright (`sets_coarsen_not_regular_absurd`). - **Theory/Universal/Element.v**: Mac Lane §III.1 def 2 + remark 3 — UNIVERSAL ELEMENTS as a first-class structure, replacing the anonymous sigma `{ x : F c & IsIsomorphism (from (Yoneda_Lemma C F c) x) }` that `Structure/UniversalProperty.v:73`'s `representability_by_yoneda` had buried inside one proposition. `UniversalElement H` (bundled) and `AUniversalElement H r` (object as parameter) carry Mac Lane's elementary unique-factorization form, with setoids comparing the underlying element. Deliverable 2 is stated at the strength it actually has: **an isomorphism of setoids in `Sets`** (`universal_element_yoneda`), not a biconditional and not mutually inverse maps up to `≈` — and it is free of transport because `ue_yoneda_obj` is `representability_by_yoneda`'s source TERM FOR TERM (the same `#[local] exists_setoid` constant, reachable by qualified name and re-registered locally; `rby_agrees` checks the match by ascription). Had a copy been re-declared instead, the two `SetoidObject`s would not have been convertible and a hand-built comparison iso would have been needed — worth knowing for anyone else composing with that proposition. The equivalence's grades are measured, not assumed: the mate's ACTION is definitional (`ue_mate_at` by `reflexivity`, so Mac Lane's `(H k) e` and the Yoneda mate's component are the same term), the passage OUT of the Yoneda encoding costs 2 obligations, and the passage IN costs 5 (the inverse must be built as a natural transformation, and each naturality square is a uniqueness argument through `fmap_comp`) — so the cheap direction is out, not in. Round trips survive on the ELEMENT by `eq_refl` both ways; the whole record does not (factorization data is rebuilt), pinned as a `Fail`. Mac Lane's remark 3 is ONE theorem, `universal_element_arrow_subsumption`, carrying both halves — (a) universal elements of `H` are universal arrows from the one-point setoid `SetsOne`, and (b) `⟨r,u⟩` is a universal arrow from `c` to `S` iff it is a universal element of `d ↦ Hom(c, S d)` — with `subsumption_composite` the round trip that exists only once both halves do. Half (b) is much the cheaper: the two records' field types are CONVERTIBLE (`fmap[[Hom c,─] ◯ S] k u` IS `fmap[S] k ∘ u`), so both passages are `:=` with no tactic and both round trips are `eq_refl` on the WHOLE record; half (a) reaches only `≈` on the arrow side, `global_element` rebuilding the map from its value at `ttt`. **A DONOR UNIVERSE RESTRICTION drove the design and is disclosed rather than worked around silently**: `Yoneda_Lemma@{u u0}` and `representability_by_yoneda@{u u0}` are stated over `C : Category@{u0 u0 u0}` — object, hom AND proof universes IDENTIFIED — so neither can be instantiated at `Instance/Coq/Nat.v`'s `Endos`, whose objects sit strictly above its homs, and the Nat witness would not have existed by that route. A hand-built Yoneda-free route (`ue_transform`/`ue_representation`/`AUniversalElement_of_repr` over the single computation `repr_eval`) carries every accessor and both `Representable` passages instead, with `routes_agree_elem` (`eq_refl`) and `routes_agree_repr` (`≈`) showing the choice costs nothing where both typecheck; three `Fail` probes pin the restriction as a genuine universe inconsistency, each against a positive control at the same arguments. The restriction is the DONOR's, not introduced here, and is NOT shown unavoidable. Payoff: `nat_UniversalElement` recovers `Instance/Coq/Nat.v:415`'s hand-checked universal element through the general class — `ue_obj = NatSucc` and `ue_elem = O` both by `eq_refl`, at the very category the Yoneda route cannot reach — and — read the attribution precisely — the statement that generalizes it is **`ue_of_repr_elem`**, over the DIRECT route with the object universe free. `universal_element_of_representation_at` does NOT: it goes through Yoneda, inherits the constraint `u = u0`, and is provably not instantiable at `Endos`, so it is a second instance of the restriction rather than an escape from it. Theory/Universal/Element/Elements.v adds Riehl 2.4.8 (`nat_Elements_Initial`: a universal element is an initial object of `Construction/Elements.v`'s category of elements), recorded on the data rather than as a setoid iso since `Initial` carries a chosen `one` field with no in-tree setoid on it. Deferred and disclosed: no dual (couniversal element / terminal object of elements); no functoriality of `UniversalElement`, no comparison with `Elements_proj`; **`Elements Endos_Forget ≃ FAlg NatF` is NOT built**, so Nat.v's `repr_initial` is neither re-derived nor made redundant — the two remain independent, and Nat.v's own stale "the tree does not carry" clause is corrected in the same commit. Prior art disclosed rather than implied away: `Structure/UniversalProperty/Universal/Arrow.v`'s `UniversalArrowIsUniversalProperty` already builds, for `AUniversalArrow c U a`, an `IsUniversalProperty` whose representing functor is the same composite `HomAfter` names — so half (b)'s CONTENT existed in the representability form, and what is new is the elementary statement and the direct passage. Two engineering findings worth carrying: a `Sets`-morphism out of the CONCRETE `SetsOne` whose `proper_morphism` is left to INSTANCE RESOLUTION — whether by `Program`'s obligation tactic or by writing `reflexive_proper` out by hand — resolves `Reflexive` at `SetsOne` and thereby pins `Sets`'s carrier universe to `Set`, making the whole of 3(a) unformable; writing the pointwise term `fun a b _ => reflexivity x` avoids the resolution and keeps it polymorphic. **It is NOT `Program`** (the same definition with a variable source stays free under `Program`), **NOT "supply the field as a term"** (`reflexive_proper _` is such a term and is pinned — only that particular pointwise term works), and **NOT `Universe Minimization ToSet`** (identical with the flag on and off). The exposure is therefore this narrow shape rather than a tree-wide hazard — a conclusion carried by the not-`Program` negative above, not by sampling; an audit sweep of 88 `Program` constants across seven `Instance/Sets*`/`Instance/Top*` modules independently found zero others pinned, which is corroboration on 7 of ~566 files and could not establish it alone, and `AUniversalElement`'s universal field takes `(d : D) (x : H d)` EXPLICITLY where `AUniversalArrow` has them implicit — deliberate, since every use site supplies both
- **Theory/Universal/Arrow/Dual.v**: Mac Lane §III.1 def 3 — COUNIVERSAL arrows, the counit-side mirror of `Theory/Universal/Arrow.v`, at the path #347 is scheduled to import rather than rebuild. The issue asked for two things that turn out to be INCOMPATIBLE at `eq_refl` strength, and the file MEASURES the incompatibility rather than quietly picking one (measured, not *proved* — a `Fail Check` records that conversion rejects the identification at the current definitions; no `≠` theorem is stated): a definitional instantiation of the primal class at the opposite categories ranges over `1 ∏ D^op`, while a terminal object of `F ↓ =(c)` ranges over `D ∏ 1`, and `Product` of categories is not symmetric on the nose. **The mismatch is NOT on the terminal/initial axis** — `terminal_is_initial_op` records by `eq_refl` that `Terminal (F ↓ =(c))` IS `Initial ((F ↓ =(c))^op)`, so the `Structure/Initial.v` idiom costs nothing here; what fails to line up is specifically WHICH comma category. The op-instantiation is taken as the DEFINITION (`CouniversalArrow c F := @UniversalArrow (C^op) (D^op) c (F^op)`, `Existing Class`, the Comonad/Core.v idiom) and the terminal reading is delivered as a proved bridge BOTH ways (`couniversal_arrow_terminal`/`couniversal_arrow_of_terminal`, the couniversal object chosen by `eq_refl`). **The covariance is real and costs nothing**: `coarrow_obj`, `coarrow`, `ump_couniversal_arrows` and `couniversal_arrow_from_UMP` are all supplied by `:=` with NO tactic, so a consumer writes `coarrow U` and `ump_couniversal_arrows U h` and never sees an `op` — the accessor discipline the issue actually asked for, verified by writing the consumer uses independently. `RightAdjointFunctorFromCouniversalArrows` and `AdjunctionFromCouniversalArrows` complete the assembly, the latter inhabiting `F ⊣ R` on the nose. Measured negatives, each pinned in Test/ProbeCouniversal.v as a `Fail` against a positive control (6 probes / 14 controls, instrument sanity-checked, each stripped and confirmed a genuine `cannot unify` or typing error): the op-side `arrow_initial` is NOT `Terminal (F ↓ =(c))`, the two comma categories are not Leibniz-equal, `CouniversalArrow c F ≠ UniversalArrow c F`, and the counit is `≈` but not `eq_refl` to `coarrow` (blocked by an `fmap[F] id` residue in the transpose, NOT by the `abstract`-opacity one might expect — the object action DOES reduce, `right_adjoint_obj` being `eq_refl`). **`Cocomma` does not help** and that is measured, not assumed: `Cocomma F =(c)` is neither the comma the op-side inhabits nor `(F ↓ =(c))^op` on the nose, both negatives pinned. NOTATION GUARD, with the halves separated (an earlier draft of this bullet overstated it): three scopes declare `_ ^op` (category/functor/adjunction) and requiring `Adjunction.Opposite` makes the adjunction one win, but the `Open Scope category_scope` is DEFENSIVE, not required — `Bind Scope category_scope with Category` rescues every `C^op` in the file, all of which sit in argument or ascription positions, and deleting the line leaves it compiling clean (measured). The hazard is real in general (a bare `Definition d := C^op.` after that Require block does fail) but this file has no such occurrence. What IS forced is the other half: `F^op` is rejected under either scope order, so every opposite functor is written `Opposite_Functor F` by name (same family as Instance/Rng/Mod.v's note). Non-vacuity is the deliberate mirror of `Adjunction/GAFT/Examples.v` (Theory/Universal/Arrow/Dual/Examples.v): the product with its projections as a couniversal arrow out of Δ, the terminal reading exercised at it, then the assembly run end to end (`Δ ⊣ R`) with `right_adjoint_iso` against `Diagonal_Product_Adjunction` showing `R ≈ ×(C)`, closing at `Sets` — and the strength runs OPPOSITE to the primal example, GAFT's functor being `Qed`-opaque where this one reduces (`fobj[R] p = fst p × snd p` by `eq_refl`). By-product beyond the issue: Arrow.v carries both encodings but never related them; the passage is added here both ways (on arrow and object, NOT as a bijection or setoid iso — disclosed). Deferred: no notation (the primal's `c ⟿ F` is section-local and does not export, so there is no spelling to mirror); the §IV.1 Thm 2 converse is #347's; no dual of `UniversalArrowIsUniversalProperty`

The components are `first f` because issue #240 prescribes them verbatim; `alt_transform` carries the `split f id` spelling alongside, related by `alt_transform_agrees`. Non-degeneracy is proven, not assumed: `fixed_product_transform_faithful` (distinct `f` give distinct transformations, no terminal object needed) and `fixed_product_transform_reflects_id`. - **Theory/Size.v**: Size and foundations vocabulary (Mac Lane §I.6, Awodey 1.11/1.12, Riehl 1.1.6/1.1.7) — `LocallySmall` and `Small`, predicates the tree had never declared (`Theory/Lawvere/Sets.v:44` records the gap). Both are relative to levels. ALL of `Small`'s content is in its strict `uo < o, uh < h`; `Test/Size.v` writes out the trivial discharge and shows it REJECTED, in two separate commands because the combined one is refused inside its hom sub-term and so says nothing about `uo < o` on its own. `LocallySmall` is deliberately NON-strict, so `locally_small_ambient` witnesses it for EVERY category — Riehl 1.1.7's "each is locally small" as a lemma, recording that `Class Category`'s `homset` field builds it in; the converse "not small" half is NOT statable, its negation being a metatheoretic claim. Objects resize up to **`ObjEq`**, a level-polymorphic identity type declared in-file — NOT stdlib `=`, which is pinned to a global universe on 8.19/8.20 and cannot be mentioned under an explicit universe-constraint clause; homs resize up to a genuine setoid isomorphism (both directions respect `≈` — `ls_from_resp` is a field because it is not derivable, and without it the structure is only a section/retraction pair). Riehl 1.1.6's unindexed packaging is `ArrowQuiver`/`ArrowQuiverOfCat`/`TotalMor` (no reflexive quiver existed in tree; the tree's `Quiver` is indexed with no identity selection), where the dom/cod retractions hold by `obj_refl` — a book axiom made definitional rather than proved; both packagings are supplied but their EQUIVALENCE is not established. **Erratum corrected**: `Check (Cat : obj[Cat])` SUCCEEDS — universe polymorphism instantiates the two occurrences at different levels, so it is the Cat/CAT tower (Riehl §1.3) rather than self-membership, and the pinned-instance form `obj[Cat@{a b c d e}] := Cat@{a b c d e}` is what is actually rejected; the `Sets` case is an ordinary type error (`obj[Sets]` is `SetoidObject`), not a universe inconsistency. Full mapping in **docs/SIZE.md**; the Awodey §8.3 Yoneda size statements are documented there but NOT formalized.

### Structures (Internal Properties)
- **Structure/Thin.v**: Thin categories — `Thin C := ∀ x y (f g : x ~> y), f ≈ g`, closed under opposites, with every morphism monic and epic and every opposing pair an iso candidate (`thin_iso`); `thin_preorder C` (inhabited-hom on objects, Prop-squashed; deliberately renamed to avoid shadowing `Theory/Category.v:282`'s exported Type-valued `hom_preorder` instance, which is the formulation that would dissolve the choice hypothesis — the squash is forced by `Instance/Proset.v`'s Prop-valued `Proset`) with its `PreOrder` witness (a plain `Definition`, deliberately not an instance). The thin-side reconstruction `thin_Proset_iso : Thin C → HomChoice C → C ≅[Cat] Proset (thin_PreOrder C)` takes the morphism-choice hypothesis explicitly (extracting a witness from the Prop-valued ∃ needs it; on prosets it is discharged, homs being the relation itself). Mac Lane §I.2 construction 7's correspondence, with strengths measured per leg in Instance/Proset/Order.v: `thin_preorder_Proset` recovers the relation pointwise-iff unconditionally, and on the nose (`thin_preorder_Proset_eq`) under propositional+functional extensionality taken as explicit hypotheses (sufficiency is what is proved; necessity is not claimed)
- **Instance/Proset/Order.v**: The order-theoretic development over `Instance/Proset.v` — Seven Sketches §§1.2.2/1.3.1 and Riehl §1.2. `Equivalence_is_PreOrder`/`PreOrder_Symmetric_is_Equivalence` (a preorder is an equivalence minus symmetry, both ways); discrete and codiscrete preorders with codiscrete terminal and discrete initial among preorder structures on a carrier (uniqueness clauses included); `comparable x y := (x ≤ y) + (y ≤ x)` with `TotalOrder` characterized by total comparability and a header note separating comparability from decidability; discrete-preorder comparability (self-comparable, distinct incomparable); `nat` a `TotalOrder` with the reverse order exhibited as `(ℕ,≤)^op`; `(Proset P)^op ≅ Proset (flip P)` in Cat (Riehl §1.2); infimum/supremum named and proved to coincide with the discrete-two-object limit/colimit over an *arbitrary preorder* (totality is needed only for existence, not the identification), two-element order worked, colimit half also restated through `IsAColimit` — stated directly; the general identification lemma belongs to issue #422 (whose suggested module is this same directory), and the header records that these are its binary-shape case, to be consumed rather than restated when #422 lands
- **Structure/Terminal.v**: Terminal and initial objects, and their uniqueness — the bundled `Terminal` class with `one`/`one_unique` and, by the `Initial C := Terminal (C^op)` notation, `Structure/Initial.v`'s `zero`/`zero_unique`; uniqueness up to a *unique* isomorphism in three strengths (`terminal_unique` + `terminal_arrow_unique`, packaged as the single `terminal_unique_up_to_unique_iso : ∃! _ : 1 ≅ 1, True` over `iso_setoid`, with the initial duals), and `zero_object_unique`/`zero_object_unique_initial` joined by the proved `zero_object_unique_compat` (Structure/ZeroObject.v). The object-level predicates `IsTerminalObj`/`IsInitialObj` (`∀ x, Unique (fun _ : x ~> c => True)`) convert both ways with the bundled classes and are what let terminality be used as a *property*: repleteness `Terminal_iso`, the full subcategory `TerminalObjects` with `TerminalObjects_Replete` and singleton hom-sets, and — given an inhabitant — `terminal_sub_contractible`, Riehl's Lemma 1.6.16 in full (`EquivalenceOfCategories (Erase (Sub C TerminalObjects))`; the "empty OR contractible" disjunction is *not* stated, since deciding it is excluded middle for "C has a terminal object" — Construction/Subcategory/Terminal.v). `Terminal_transport`/`Initial_transport` carry either object along an equivalence (Theory/Equivalence/Terminal.v). Riehl Exercise 1.6.i gives `terminal_initial_arrow_iso` and with it `ZeroObject_from_arrow`, the first in-tree construction that *derives* `zero_coincide` rather than assuming it. Terminality as a universal property is `TerminalIsUniversalProperty`/`InitialIsUniversalProperty` — the instance `Structure/UniversalProperty.v`'s `univ_property_unique_up_to_unique_iso` had always lacked at its own illustrating case — with `terminal_unique_up_to_unique_iso_via_UP` re-deriving the concrete statement through it (Structure/UniversalProperty/Terminal.v). Strict initiality is `initial_strict : (x ~> 0) → x ≅ 0` with `initial_strict_unique` (Structure/BiCCC.v — cartesian closed + initial suffices, no `Cocartesian`), instantiated at `Sets` and at skeletal `FinSet`, where it computes to `∀ x : nat, (x ~> 0) → x = 0` (Structure/BiCCC/Strict.v)
- **Structure/Cartesian.v**: Products via universal property
- **Structure/Cartesian/Closed.v**: Exponentials and internal hom (the live
  cartesian-closed development; `Structure/Closed.v` is an incomplete
  Eilenberg–Kelly stub whose `Class Closed` is not yet in force). The currying
  adjunction is packaged in Structure/Cartesian/Closed/Adjunction.v: the
  endofunctors `Prod_Functor S = (− × S)` and `Exp_Functor S = (−)^S` (the
  latter the partial application of Functor/Hom/Internal.v's internal-hom
  bifunctor, `Exp_Functor_InternalHom`), the named naturality lemma
  `eval_natural` with its bundled `eval_Transform : (−)^S × S ⟹ Id`, and
  `Curry_Adjunction : Prod_Functor S ⊣ Exp_Functor S` in the hom-set form of
  Theory/Adjunction.v — `curry`/`uncurry` as the transposes and `eval` as
  counit, all three by `reflexivity`, with the triangle identities inherited
  from the general theory; this turns the prose remark of Theory/Adjunction.v
  (lines 93-94) into a theorem. `Curry_Representable` is the first instance of
  `Functor/Representable.v`'s class (and its first consumer: resolution finds
  it, and the class's `Representable_to_obj` coercion reads the representing
  object back), witnessing Riehl's Example 2.1.6(iv) — the presheaf
  `C(− × S, B) : C^op ⟶ Sets` is represented by `B^S`, with the Yoneda
  universal element `eval` (`Curry_repr_universal`). That is a claim about
  THAT class: representability content also lives under
  `Structure/UniversalProperty.v`'s `IsUniversalProperty`, which has three
  instances. Note also that the counit's being `eval` on the nose is a
  consequence of the transposes alone, not of defining the exponential
  endofunctor directly — what the direct definition buys is that the four
  naturality fields discharge from the `Closed` corollaries verbatim
  (audit-corrected). The `Sets` model, where the counit computes to
  `e(h, s) = h s` and the transpose of addition on a `nat` setoid witnesses
  non-vacuity, is Instance/Sets/Cartesian/Closed/Adjunction.v, which also
  carries the Mac Lane §V.4 corollary that `− × X` preserves all colimits
  (`Sets_prod_preserves_colimits`, `CCC_prod_preserves_colimits`) — the
  library's only colimit-preservation witness of this kind
- **Structure/Cartesian/Closed/Natural.v**: Naturality of the exponential laws (Mac Lane §II.5 Ex 2; Riehl §1.4 Example 1.4.11) — the four pointwise isos upgraded to natural isomorphisms with their sides packaged as eight DIRECT functor records over product/opposite domains: `exp_prod_l_natural : @Isomorphism ([(C^op ∏ C^op) ∏ C, C]) …` and `exp_prod_r_natural` over Cartesian+Closed alone, `prod_coprod_r_natural`/`exp_coprod_natural` after a mid-section Cocartesian Context (so the first two never carry it). The components ARE the existing `exp_prod_l`/`exp_prod_r`/`prod_coprod_r`/`exp_coprod` DEFINITIONALLY — eight `reflexivity` agreement lemmas (stated with `≈`, morphisms) plus eight `eq_refl` object-action Examples (objects) — so the upgrade adds naturality, not new components. The transpose triangle on ARBITRARY generalized elements relates the internal iso to `exp_iso`'s legs: `exp_prod_l_transpose : to exp_prod_l ∘ f ≈ curry (curry (uncurry f ∘ to prod_assoc))` and `_untranspose` dually. Two per-variable SPECIMEN corollaries — the joint square instantiated at identity arrows, covering Law 1's base variable and Law 4's first summand only, the file saying so — since the joint squares imply every per-variable square; `NaturalIso_of_pointwise` (pointwise family + one square ⟹ natural iso) and the `ihom` calculus stated for reuse (`ihom` IS `InternalHomFunctor`'s fmap read in C, machine-checked at Leibniz `eq_refl` by `ihom_is_InternalHomFunctor_fmap`); `prod_assoc_to_natural`/`_from_natural` general but disclosed as not consumed by the squares. Engineering finding recorded in-file: an inline `fobj` inside `Program Definition` lets Program defer an unresolved `Cartesian` instance into an obligation that `Unset Transparent Obligations` (Lib/Foundation.v) makes OPAQUE — `fobj[F]` then converts with nothing; elaborate object/arrow actions as ordinary Definitions first. The Cat note is Instance/Cat/Exponential.v: all four pointwise isos AND all four `_natural` upgrades instantiate at Cat with explicitly spelled functor-category types (`[C ∏ D, E] ≅ [C, [D, E]]`, `[C, D ∏ E] ≅ [C, D] ∏ [C, E]`, `C ∏ (D ∐ E) ≅ (C ∏ D) ∐ (C ∏ E)`, `[D ∐ E, C] ≅ [D, C] ∏ [E, C]`), inhabited by the generic instances so the two readings agree by conversion — no universe wall. The decategorification half (Riehl's second clause) is Instance/FinSet/Decategorify.v: `Card_Groupoid : Groupoid Set_f ⟶ DiscreteCat nat` — THE cardinality functor from the groupoid of finite sets and bijections to the discrete category of naturals (fobj `setf_cardinality`, fmap `setf_cardinality_iso_invariant`, which forgets WHICH bijection — that forgetting IS decategorification; laws by axiom-free `UIP_dec` on nat) — with the identity-on-objects skeletal shadow `Card_FinSet : Groupoid FinSet ⟶ DiscreteCat nat` (fmap := `FinSet_skeletal`), and the four arithmetic identities DERIVED from the categorical isomorphisms — each PROOF TERM is `λ x y z, FinSet_skeletal <the iso>` with no arithmetic induction of its own (the counting lives inside `FinSet_skeletal`'s pigeonhole engine and FinSet's instance layer, per the file's disclosure; none of `lia`/`Nat.pow_*`/`Nat.mul_add_distr_l` appears): `nat_exp_prod_l : z^(x·y) = (z^y)^x`, `nat_exp_prod_r : (y·z)^x = y^x·z^x`, `nat_prod_coprod_r : x·(y+z) = x·y+x·z`, `nat_exp_coprod : x^(y+z) = x^y·x^z`, each literally `FinSet_skeletal` of the corresponding iso at skeletal FinSet (whose product/coproduct/exponential object actions are `*`/`+`/`^` on nat), with `nat_exp_coprod_is_Card_image` identifying one as `fmap[Card_FinSet]` of the iso by `eq_refl`, `DiscreteCat_nat_Thin` recording that the naturality content evaporates in the discrete target, and the header disclosing that `lia` would prove the identities — the derivation route is the content. Every `=` in that file is between OBJECTS (nats) or is a `DiscreteCat` hom (an equality proof), the setoid discipline's sanctioned exceptions
- **Structure/Monoidal.v**: Tensor products with coherence
- **Structure/Monoidal/CopyDiscard.v**: Copy/discard (gs-monoidal) categories — comonoid supply with no naturality; deterministic morphisms and the wide subcategory Det in CopyDiscard/Deterministic.v
- **Structure/Monoidal/Markov.v**: Markov categories (copy/discard + semicartesian); Fox's theorem in Markov/Fox.v (all-deterministic ⟺ cartesian)
- **Structure/Premonoidal.v**: Premonoidal categories over Structure/Binoidal.v — centre Z(C) as a monoidal wide subcategory (Binoidal/Central.v, Premonoidal/Centre.v), Monoidal ⟺ all-central premonoidal (Premonoidal/Monoidal.v), Freyd/effectful categories (Premonoidal/Freyd.v); Kleisli premonoidal structure and thunkability in Monad/Kleisli/{Premonoidal,Commutative}.v and Monad/Thunkable.v; graded monads in Monad/Graded.v; strict premonoidal = monoid in (StrictCat, □) in Instance/StrictCat/Premonoid.v
- **Structure/Monoidal/Drinfeld.v**: The Drinfeld (monoidal) centre `Z(C)` — `HalfBraiding` (a natural family satisfying the half-braiding hexagon), objects `(x, half-braiding)` with intertwiner morphisms (proof-irrelevant witness), `Drinfeld_Monoidal`, `Drinfeld_Braided` (a braided monoidal category with BOTH hexagon identities at full strength), and the forgetful `Drinfeld_Forget`; this is the monoidal centre, distinct from the premonoidal centre of Binoidal/Central + Premonoidal/Centre
- **Structure/Monoidal/StarAutonomous.v**: Star-autonomous categories (definition level) over a genuine general base `SymMonClosed` — symmetric monoidal closed, introduced in-file because the in-tree `ClosedMonoidal` bundles `CartesianMonoidal` (over a cartesian closed base a dualizing object forces a preorder by Joyal's no-go, excluding Rel/FdVect/coherence spaces); provides the dual/linear-negation functor `(- ⇒ ⊥) : C^op ⟶ C`, the double-dual endofunctor, and the `StarAutonomous` class (dualizing object, closed transpose, double-dual iso with naturality), with par/linear-distributivity and the canonicity coherence ledgered
- **Structure/Limit.v**: Limits and colimits — with the transfer vocabulary alongside: preservation and iso-reflection in Structure/Limit/Preservation.v, and CREATION in Structure/Limit/Creation.v. The cone-level predicate `IsLimitCone` (a given cone is universal) is what the apex-only `IsALimit` cannot express and what `PreservesImageLimit` (Construction/Comma/Limit.v) had been writing out by hand — the two bridges are proof-free repackagings whose round trips are `eq_refl` (Construction/Comma/Creation.v), so GAFT and SAFT already consume it. `CreatesLimit K F` carries three fields (a lift of every limiting cone downstairs, a cone isomorphism from its image, and reflection); Mac Lane §V.1's other two clauses are theorems, not fields — `creates_limiting` (the lift is limiting) and `creates_lift_unique` (any cone lying over the given one is canonically isomorphic to the lift, uniqueness in the form a setoid library supports). `StrictlyCreatesLimit` is Riehl §3.4.7's on-the-nose variant over `StrictLift` (object `=` on the apex, `≈` on the legs), with `StrictlyCreatesLimit_CreatesLimit` relating them. Mac Lane §V.4 Theorem 2 in both halves: `creation_preserves_limit` (cone-level, with the downstairs limit as an explicit hypothesis, per Riehl's footnote) and `creates_limits_Complete`/`creates_limits_continuous`, plus creation under composition and the colimit duals by `C^op`. Witnesses: `EM_Forget` strictly creates every limit and limits of algebras are computed on carriers (Monad/Eilenberg/Moore/Limit.v, with `EM_Complete` proving Structure/Complete.v:58's standing prose, and an axiom-free instantiation at `Id_Monad` over `Coq`); `comma_proj2` given `Complete C` (Construction/Comma/Creation.v, its lift agreeing with the chosen downstairs limit by `eq_refl` on both apex and legs); and every equivalence, hence `Id[C]` (Theory/Equivalence/Creation.v), which is also how `equivalence_creates_limits` finally earns its name
- **Structure/Pullback/Reduction.v**, **Structure/Pullback/Wide.v**: The classical interdefinability of the finite (co)limit generators (Mac Lane §III.3 Ex 2, §III.4 Ex 7/9/10; Awodey §5.2 Prop 5.7 + Cor 5.8, §5.4 Prop 5.16, §5.7 Ex 3/5; Riehl 2nd ed. §3.5 Lemmas 3.5.15/3.5.16) — the reduction toolkit `Structure/Topos.v` had disclosed as unformalized since it was written, and the two `jww` TODOs `Structure/Pullback.v` had carried since 2017, both retired in the same commit. Every reduction is stated FIRST at the apex-pinned level (`IsPullback`/`IsEqualizer`/`IsCartesianProduct`/`IsIndexedProduct`) and only then packaged: `pullback_of_equalizer` with `HasPullbacks_of_Cartesian_HasEqualizers`, the Awodey converse `equalizer_of_pullback` (which deliberately does NOT use `equalizer_monic` — the pullback's own uniqueness clause discharges the factorization directly, disclosed in-header as a departure from Awodey's argument), the kernel pair as an equalizer (`kernel_pair_IsEqualizer`, tied to the PRE-EXISTING `Structure/Regular.v:46` `kernel_pair` by `kernel_pair_is_pullback_along_itself` rather than shadowing it), `product_of_pullback`/`Cartesian_of_HasPullbacks_Terminal`/`HasEqualizers_of_HasPullbacks_Terminal`, and the dual `HasPushouts_of_Cocartesian_HasCoequalizers`. **The issue's own framing was WRONG and the file proves it rather than repeating it**: Mac Lane's Ex 9 and the Awodey/Riehl construction are NOT "the same square read the other way" — Mac Lane pulls back `⟨id,f⟩` and `⟨id,g⟩`, BOTH issuing from `x`, over `x × y`, while Awodey and Riehl pull the diagonal `y ~> y × y` back along `⟨f,g⟩`. The refutation comes in TWO KINDS kept lexically apart: the codomains differ (CONVERSION), and the legs are not even parallel so the comparison CANNOT BE STATED (FORMABILITY — `aw_pair f g : x ~> y × y` against `x ~> x × y`). Structurally, both Mac Lane legs are split monic with the one common retraction `exl`, whereas `exl ∘ aw_pair` is `f`, which is not an identity FOR GENERAL `f` — a hedge that matters, since at `f := id` Awodey's leg IS split, by this file's own `ml_right_split`; the lemma was renamed `aw_pair_retracts_to_f` because its first name, `aw_pair_not_split`, asserted more than it proves and more than is true. What IS proved is the weaker positive statement: the two vertices are canonically isomorphic AS EQUALIZERS (`maclane_awodey_iso`, `maclane_awodey_iso_commutes` — commuting with BOTH equalizing maps, not merely an iso of objects), with one degenerate definitional coincidence at `f := id`. It is NOT proved that no cospan isomorphism exists at particular `f`, `g`, and the header says so. **The duality is measured, not assumed**: `HasCoequalizers C = HasEqualizers (C^op)` is REFUTED at `eq_refl` (records are nominal) — yet every FIELD type IS convertible, so both bridges are `:=` with no tactic and both record round trips close by `eq_refl`; the residue is exactly TWO steps, `HasEqualizers_op_of_HasCoequalizers` and its mirror `HasCoequalizers_of_HasEqualizers_op`, each destructing the `sigT` (an earlier draft said one, overlooking the second in the same section). `Coprod`/`inl`/`inr` are the op-product/`exl`/`exr` by `eq_refl`, so section (A) applies at `C^op` verbatim. Wide.v adds `IsWidePullback`, `WidePullback`, `HasWidePullbacks` (declared but UNINHABITED) and Riehl's Lemma 3.5.15 in BOTH directions (`wide_pullback_product`/`product_wide_pullback`), with `one_unique` VISIBLE as a single named step — discharging the commutativity condition forward, manufacturing it backward. **The empty index comes out right, and for a structural reason**: the terminal object IS the empty wide pullback, proved both ways and genuinely consuming the emptiness — the opposite outcome from the sibling `Structure/Equalizer/Wide.v`, whose empty case is an obstruction RELATIVE TO ITS LIMIT PRESENTATION, and no pointed section or `i0` hypothesis appears here. **Read the contrast narrowly.** An earlier draft of this bullet explained it by saying a wide fork "carries a second leg the empty condition leaves unconstrained" — that is FALSE about that file's record (`IsWideEqualizer` has two fields and ONE leg, and that file says three times that no leg-carrying record is built there), and the polarity was inverted: the fork's LACK of a second leg is why `i0` is needed, the cone's second leg being MANUFACTURED as `fs i0 ∘ h`. The honest statement is weaker — this file builds NO limit presentation, so no analogous disagreement can arise, and the contrast partly reflects that less is claimed here rather than that the notion is better behaved. Non-vacuity at `Sets_HasIndexedProducts`. SCOPE, and it is narrower than "finite limits are redundant": only the BINARY and PARALLEL-PAIR generators are related. No finite-shape induction is performed, so `Complete`/`Cocomplete` is NOT concluded and Riehl 3.5.15 is not stated for arbitrary finite shape; (E)'s hypotheses are not shown minimal and have no converse; the produced `Cartesian` is not compared with any pre-existing one on the same category; `IsWidePullback` has NO limit presentation (nothing relates it to a `Limit` over a wide-cospan shape, so there is no analogue of `Structure/Pullback/Limit.v`); `wide_pullback_unique` gives a bare `≅` with no leg equations; and Reduction.v makes no universe claim at all. `Test/ProbeReduction.v` guards the boundary — 8 negatives (3 conversion in the D-section, 2 formability, 2 duality, 1 wide) plus a separate instrument check, against controls naming their own constants, each negative stripped and its failure KIND confirmed. The rename guarantee was NOT true as first shipped — an independent audit found `ml_right`, `HasCoequalizers` and `HasEqualizers` named in no control, so renaming any of them left the file compiling and its negative vacuously green; controls for all three were added, the same defect family two earlier probe files in this tree shipped with. Wide.v's three universe measurements were likewise measured-but-unguarded and are now shipped as probe sections, the donor `IsIndexedProduct` rejected at the same levels so that "inherited" is measured rather than asserted; Wide.v ships no `Fail` of its own, so its refutations are pinned there for the first time

- **Structure/Limit/Unique.v**: Riehl §3.1 Proposition 3.1.7 — essential uniqueness of limits WITH the leg equations, from the mediator calculus alone (`limit_med`/`limit_med_commutes`/`limit_med_eq`, no representability detour): `limit_unique_iso : c ≅ d` between any two `IsALimit F` witnesses, `limit_unique_iso_legs` carrying both leg-commutation families (the reviewer bar: not a bare `≅`), `limit_unique_iso_unique` (ANY morphism commuting with the two leg families is the isomorphism — the proposition's content), and the automorphism corollary `limit_endo_id`; the colimit statements are literal op-instances of the limit ones re-typed through the covariant `colimit_inj` vocabulary (`colimit_unique_iso`/`_injs`/`_unique`, `colimit_endo_id`; the mediator is definitionally the opposite side's, `colimit_unique_to_is_op`) — not a second proof. Structure/Limit/Unique/Compat.v ties the pre-existing routes to it: `equalizer_unique` and `pullback_unique` AGREE with the general isomorphism — componentwise and at iso level (`equalizer_unique_agrees`/`_iso`, `pullback_unique_agrees`/`_iso`) — through the in-tree bridges (`is_equalizer_limit`, `Pullback_from_Universal`); their terminating Qeds became Defineds for exactly this comparison, no proof text changed; so the general theorem strictly subsumes both (adding legs + uniqueness the bare `≅`s lacked); and the generic route — in tree instantiated only at terminal/initial objects — is taken at limits for the first time (`limit_unique_up_to_transport_iso` := `univ_property_unique_up_to_unique_iso` at `LimitIsUniversalProperty`), with the transport-vs-leg-precomposition identification — which would compare the generic isomorphism componentwise — disclosed as the remaining open gap (the hint-automation-built representation equivalence has no leg characterisation in tree)
- **Structure/Limit/Initial.v**: Mac Lane §III.4 Exercise 3 (book p. 72) + Seven Sketches §3.5.3 Ex 3.98 + Riehl §3.1 — **limits computed by EVALUATION when the INDEX category has an initial object**. `initial_IsALimit I F : IsALimit F (F initial_obj)` with legs `initial_leg := fmap[F] zero`, the mediator out of a competing cone being that cone's own leg at the initial object (`initial_med N := cone_leg N initial_obj`), plus `initial_IsLimitCone` at cone level, `initial_Limit`, and the terminal-cone reading `initial_Cones_Terminal : @Terminal (Cones F)`. **Nothing in tree previously constrained the INDEXING category's own initial object** — `rg 'Initial J\b|@Initial J\b' -g '*.v'` returns 0 hits, which the file header is careful to scope as a search over the SPELLING of the hypothesis and not over its meaning, so it does not rule out an equivalent statement phrased another way, every prior `initial object … limit` hit concerning the initial object of the CONE/comma/factorization category or of the TARGET. **The dual is genuinely covariant**: `terminal_IsAColimit T F : IsAColimit F (F terminal_obj)` and its siblings are routed through `C^op` but **no `^op` occurs in any type in that section**, the four packagings and both accessors being `:=` with no tactic. **NO `Limit F ↔ Initial J` IS CLAIMED** — that converse is neither proved nor asserted; the honest biconditional delivered instead is `limiting_iff_initial_leg_iso : IsLimitCone N ↔ IsIsomorphism (cone_leg N initial_obj)`, both directions proved, with `colimiting_iff_terminal_inj_iso` dual. **BOTH APPENDED-CHECKBOX DONORS HAD TO BE BUILT, AND THAT IS MEASURED**: `Cat_Terminal` (`Instance/One.v:58`) makes `_1` the terminal object OF CAT, which is NOT `_1` having an initial object WITHIN itself, so `One_Initial : @Initial _1` is new here; and `rg 'Terminal.*Ordinal|ord_top'` returned 0 hits, so `Ordinal_Succ_Terminal n : @Terminal (Ordinal (S n))` and `ord_top` are new too — each verified to exist in no other file. On those: Seven Sketches Ex 3.98 is `point_IsALimit F : IsALimit F (F ttt)` as the SPECIAL CASE of the headline, cross-checked against the terminal-cone definition by `point_Cones_strict` (`eq_refl`), with a `Sets` witness whose mediator COMPUTES (`point_Sets_med b : limit_med … (elt_cone b) ttt = b` by `eq_refl`) — though that identification holds at ARBITRARY arguments (`initial_med_strict`), so instantiating it proves nothing by itself and the content is `point_Sets_separates`, which shows the apex has two distinct elements (by `discriminate`, the mediator being definitionally the leg — it is not the universal property that separates); Riehl's dual is `ordinal_succ_IsAColimit G : IsAColimit G (G (ord_top n))`. **RIEHL'S ω+1 IS NOT INSTANTIATED and the file says so** — no ω+1 category exists in tree, so what is delivered is finite `Ordinal (S n)` together with `Omega_Initial : @Initial Omega` and `Omega_no_Terminal : @Terminal Omega → False`, which is the half of Riehl's remark that ω itself supplies; nothing claims `Ordinal (S n)` IS ω+1. Universes: `initial_IsALimit`/`initial_Limit` identify the index category's hom-and-proof universe with the ambient category's (`u0 = u2`) while **both OBJECT universes stay free**, and the identification has **at least THREE co-equal donors, two of them CONE VOCABULARY** — with the levels declared apart `ACone`/`Cone` elaborate while `cone_leg` (`Structure/Limit/Preservation.v:108`), `IsLimitCone` (`:166`) and `IsALimit` (`Structure/Limit.v:129`) are ALL rejected with the same `Cannot enforce jp = jh`; an earlier revision of this bullet blamed `IsALimit` alone on the strength of the `ACone` control, which rules out only `ACone`/`Cone` and licenses no such conclusion, and `IsALimit` is not even the donor met FIRST (`initial_med` already carries `u0 = u2` while its type mentions no `IsALimit`, its body being `cone_leg N initial_obj`, and `initial_leg` carries only the bound `u0 <= u2`).
