- **Instance/Top/FundamentalGroupoid.v**: The fundamental groupoid π(X) — objects are the points of X, arrows are homotopy classes rel endpoints of paths, composition is reparametrized concatenation and inversion is path reversal; `FundamentalGroupoid X : Category`, `fundamental_groupoid_is_groupoid`, `fundamental_group X a` as the vertex group, and Riehl Corollary 1.5.14 as `fundamental_group_basepoint_independent` — derived THROUGH the connected-groupoid structure theorem, not by an independent conjugation argument. All four category and groupoid laws are instances of ONE construction, `straight_homotopy` H(t,s) = p((1−s)·φ(t) + s·ψ(t)), reducing to four pointwise identities over the reparametrizations of `Instance/Top/Interval.v` (`rf_assoc` = min(2t, t+1/4, t/2+1/2)); well-definedness on classes (`path_concat_respects`) is separate, pasting two homotopies across the square. `Instance/Top/Interval.v` supplies [0,1] and the square over the STANDARD LIBRARY REALS through `BallSpace`/`BallTop` (radius is DATA, which discharges Top's Type-valued union axiom with no choice), plus `paste_arrow`; its `Rcases`/`Rlin` are deliberately GLOBAL `Ltac`s rather than `Lib/Tactics.v` residents, since moving them would hand the reals' axioms to the whole tree — disclosed in the header, along with the fact that no `.glob` name sweep sees an `Ltac`. **These, with Instance/Top/Presheaf.v, Instance/Top/ContinuousRing.v, Instance/Top/Homotopy.v and Instance/Top/LoopSpace.v, together with the three metric-space files (`Instance/Met.v`, `Instance/Met/Extended.v`, `Instance/Met/Completion.v`), are the only nine DEVELOPMENT files in the tree that import the reals (`Test/ProbeMet.v` imports them too, so an unrestricted `rg` returns ten)**, and the axiom price is measured PER CONSTANT, not sampled: at most two stdlib axioms (`sig_forall_dec`, `functional_extensionality_dep`), plus a third (`sig_not_dec`) on exactly EIGHT constants running through the least-upper-bound property — `gval_endpoints`, `f_endpoints`, `interval_to_discrete_constant_dec` with its two-point corollary `interval_to_discrete_constant`, and `no_path_true_false`, `Bool_Discrete_not_pathconnected`, `Bool_Discrete_pi_not_connected`, `Bool_Discrete_loops_trivial`. Two is a CEILING, not an invariable charge: of Interval.v's 160 constants 1 is closed (`rf_id`), 34 carry only `sig_forall_dec`, 125 carry both; of FundamentalGroupoid.v's 113 — the 108 in the `.glob` PLUS five `FundamentalGroupoid_obligation_*` that no glob sweep sees — 1 is closed, 1 carries one, 103 carry two, 8 carry three; all enumerated in docs/AXIOMS.md. Non-vacuity separates two topologies on ONE set: `Bool_Discrete` and `TwoPoint_Indiscrete` share `bool_setoid_object` (`bool_carriers_agree` by `eq_refl`) and π tells them apart, the discrete half resting on `interval_to_discrete_constant_dec` — every continuous map from [0,1] into ANY discrete space with DECIDABLE equality is constant. The decider is a hypothesis, so no axiom is incurred; it is stated with `sum` rather than `sumbool` and supplies a `Prop` shadow of `≈`, both forced by this library's `≈` being `crelation`-valued while `completeness` needs a `Prop`. Limits disclosed: NO space with nontrivial π₁ is exhibited (both witnesses have trivial vertex groups at EVERY base point, both ∀-quantified and proved), so the pair is a contrast in CONNECTEDNESS and nothing else; π₁(S¹) ≅ ℤ needs covering-space theory; connectedness of [0,1] in the general sense (no separation by disjoint nonempty opens) is still unproven; and Riehl Remark 1.5.15 is formalized COMPONENTWISE only. docs/INHABITATION.md carries the row and the note
- **Instance/Top/Homotopy.v**: Homotopy categories and pointed spaces (Mac Lane §I.7 constructions 5+7, pp. 25-26; §II.8 remark 1; Riehl §1.1 Ex 1.1.iv) — homotopy of continuous maps WITHOUT products in Top: the cylinder-as-object is UNIVERSE-OBSTRUCTED (measured by probe, recorded in the header: opens live one universe above points, and the product-rectangle condition quantifies over opens of X, so `Cyl X` is a `TopSpace` only one level up — the Forgetful.v stratification met from the other side), so `Homotopy f g` is a RAW-CERTIFICATE record (map + properness + joint-continuity certificate stated as the pointwise rectangle condition + endpoint equations), Type-valued DATA over the Type-valued `HomRelT` — Riehl clause (ii)'s presentational point resolved in the strong direction, every congruence clause a CONSTRUCTION on witnesses. The four `HomCongruence` fields: constant track, time reversal, concatenation (`homotopy_trans` — paste_arrow's seam argument REDONE at certificate level, three-way `total_order_T` with opens intersected and radii halved at the seam; paste_arrow itself needs a BallSpace domain an arbitrary X lacks), and the DIAGONAL composition `H1(H2 x t, t)` (no cylinder functoriality, no transitivity needed — the outer certificate hands out an open of the middle space, exactly what the inner consumes). **`Toph := Quotient Top homotopic`** over the NAMED `homotopy_congruence` (the II.8 checkbox: the in-tree hom-congruence quotient, not a bespoke category), `TophProj` full. **`Top_pointed`** (pointed spaces, hom-setoid INHERITED from Top on underlying maps — restating it would sit a universe too low, and inheritance makes based clauses hand hypotheses straight to unbased ones) is CLOSED under the global context — pointed spaces mention no interval; **`Toph_pointed`** over `based_homotopy_congruence`, with basedness THROUGHOUT the track (`bhtpy_base` quantifies over every t, per the reviewer note). Witnesses: the straight-line contraction (`interval_id_homotopic_const` with `interval_id_not_const` — Toph identifies what Top separates, Mac Lane's "arrows are not functions" moral), based variant, and the capstone BEYOND the issue: homotopy equivalences ARE Toph's isomorphisms both ways (`homotopy_equivalence_Toph_iso`/`Toph_iso_homotopy_equivalence` — repackagings, the dividend of building Toph as a Quotient) with **`interval_iso_point_in_Toph`** against `interval_not_iso_point_in_Top` — the interval is a point in Toph and provably not in Top. Circle/disc non-monic example deferred-disclosed (needs genuine algebraic topology); the finer rel-endpoints `ArrowHomotopy` of FundamentalGroupoid.v explicitly distinguished. Axiom footprint measured per constant in docs/AXIOMS.md (17 Closed incl the whole `Top_pointed` spine / 60 with exactly the two-axiom ceiling / ZERO `sig_not_dec` — completeness never touched); no docs/INHABITATION.md row — unlike the π-development's conditional notes, the flagships here are unconditional with in-tree witnesses; the equivalence/iso passages are mutually inverse on the nose (`homotopy_equivalence_round`/`Toph_iso_round`, Leibniz round trips)
- **Instance/Top/LoopSpace.v**: Loop interchange and the abelian fundamental group of a topological group (Mac Lane §II.5 Exercises 4+6) — the second in-tree consumer of Theory/EckmannHilton.v, and the classical one. `HTopMonoid` (a `TopSpace` with a unit point, `Proper` multiplication, STRICT unit laws, and joint continuity as the two-open rectangle certificate — Top has no products, the Homotopy.v accommodation) is EXACTLY what the exercises consume, so the theorem is stated at H-space-with-strict-unit strength; `TopGroup` extends it (inverse + laws + `tg_inv_cont`, literally continuity of inversion) so Mac Lane's named object exists, with the honest note that nothing on the path to the theorem uses the inverse. `mult_arrow` develops the pointwise product ONCE over an arbitrary domain `TopSpace` — at `I_Top` it multiplies paths, at `Sq_Top` homotopies, and it mentions no reals at all; `loop_mult` with `loop_mult_respects` and strict unit laws lifted by FundamentalGroupoid.v's existing `pointwise_homotopic`. Exercise 4 is `loop_interchange : (α ∗ β) · (γ ∗ δ) ∼ (α · γ) ∗ (β · δ)` — a COMPUTATION, not surgery: both sides split at 1/2 identically, so pointwise equality plus `pointwise_homotopic` closes it. Matching Exercise 4's displayed law variable-for-variable assigns f := pointwise product, g := concatenation (the swapped assignment is NOT false — interchange is self-dual under swapping f and g with the middle arguments transposed, and would deliver the same conclusions from the f-side), so π₁-abelian comes out as the g-side corollary `eh_g_comm`: `pi1_topgroup_abelian : mon_op p q ≈ mon_op q p` at `fundamental_group G (htm_unit G)` (pinned name, over `HTopMonoid`), `pi1_TopGroup_abelian` the Mac Lane reading, `loop_concat_comm` the loop-level form, plus `loop_mult_concat_agree`/`loop_mult_comm`/`loop_mult_assoc` read off the same instantiation, and the unmisreadability anchors `pi1_mon_op_is_concat`/`pi1_mon_unit_is_const` by `eq_refl`. Witness: `R_TopMonoid`/`R_TopGroup` ((ℝ,+) with ε/2 certificates) and `pi1_R_abelian`, disclosed as exercising the hypotheses only (π₁(ℝ) trivial; no in-tree space has nontrivial π₁). Axiom footprint per constant in docs/AXIOMS.md: of 52 constants, 23 CLOSED — the ENTIRE abstract spine, both records, all projections, `open_of_nbhds` (an upstreaming candidate: a predicate with an open neighbourhood at each point is open) and all of `mult_arrow` — 27 at the two-axiom ceiling, 2 `sig_forall_dec` alone, ZERO `sig_not_dec`; `pi1_topgroup_abelian` itself carries exactly the two. Ω(G,e) as a SPACE (compact-open) is out of scope, disclosed
- **Instance/Met.v**: Mac Lane §III.1 construction 3 + Seven Sketches §2.3.3 Definition 2.51 — metric spaces, their completion as a UNIVERSAL ARROW, and extended metric spaces, across `Instance/Met.v` (the category), `Instance/Met/Extended.v` (the `[0,∞]` variant) and `Instance/Met/Completion.v` (the construction). `MetricSpace` carries Seven Sketches' four axioms with NON-NEGATIVITY DERIVED rather than assumed, so it has one field fewer than the book's ℝ≥0-codomain presentation; morphisms are ISOMETRIES, which is what the catalogued item asks for ("metric-preserving functions (necessarily injective)"), and Mac Lane's parenthetical is proved — `isometry_injective`, with `Met_all_Monic` as its categorical shadow. The header records that Mac Lane uses a DIFFERENT category one chapter later (§IV.3's reflective-subcategory example, over uniformly continuous maps) and that no comparison between the two is built. **The codomain is stdlib ℝ and that is forced, not chosen**: the completion's distance is `lim d(xₙ,yₙ)`, so the codomain must contain the limits of its own Cauchy sequences — ℚ does not, so a ℚ-valued `Met` is not closed under the very construction being formalized and the universal arrow would be unstatable. Two halves are kept apart: `cdist_spec` and `cdist_unique` MACHINE-CHECK that the distance is the limit and that any real the distances converge to IS it (so the limit is not one admissible distance among several), while the claim that ℚ lacks such limits is ARGUED and labelled as argued — `Search "irrational"` over the loaded stdlib returns nothing, so there is no result to lean on. `CMet` is the complete full subcategory with `CMet_Incl` full and faithful; the completion is by Cauchy sequences with **the modulus carried as DATA** (the tree's `∃` is `sigT`), which is precisely what makes the diagonal completeness argument choice-free — extracting thresholds from `Prop` existentials would be countable choice. `Completion_AUniversalArrow` is the headline, with `completion_unique` giving uniqueness up to unique isomorphism CARRYING the leg equation, obtained from `Theory/Universal/Arrow.v`'s `auniversal_arrow_unique` rather than by hand. **A pre-existing tree defect was surfaced in the attempt**: `Structure/UniversalProperty/Universal/Arrow.v`'s `UniversalArrowIsUniversalProperty` — the intended route — is not instantiable at any of the tree's LARGE concrete categories, `Sets` and `Coq` and the wanted `CMet_Incl` alike, each rejected by a universe inconsistency; it is NOT uninstantiable in general, since it elaborates at `_1`, `_2` and `Cat`, so the defect is a size boundary rather than a blanket failure. It has no consumer anywhere in the tree, so this is the first attempt to give it one; the obstruction is not this development's and is pinned as a probe so its repair gets noticed. Non-vacuity is proved on both sides: `Harmonic_not_MComplete` (an explicit modulus with no limit — and since `MComplete` is the WEAKER Bishop-style property, refuting it is the STRONGER negative, so the space is classically incomplete too) and `eta_Harmonic_not_surjective` (a point of the completion outside the embedding's image). The extended-metric box is DELIVERED: `Rinf`, `ExtMetricSpace` on the same four axioms, both passages, and `metric_iff_finite_extended` stated over a FIXED carrier (comparing records with different carriers would need a contentless transport). Strengths measured strict-first — `R_Metric_dist`, the metric↔extended round trip on the metric side, and `Completion_carrier` close by `eq_refl`; the three that do NOT are pinned as rejection probes with positive controls, the sharpest being that `cdist (eta a) (eta b) = dist a b` is not definitional because it needs uniqueness of limits. Axiom cost measured PER CONSTANT across all 184 (docs/AXIOMS.md): mentioning `R` is free, real arithmetic costs `sig_forall_dec` (+ `functional_extensionality_dep`), and only `R_complete` adds `sig_not_dec` — which in `Met.v` is exactly two constants and in `Extended.v` is none. **These files are deliberately NOT in the `print-assumptions` gate**, which fails on any axiom but the ZX `Phase` parameters; none of the six pre-existing reals files is in it either. Nothing here computes (`cdist` is a `proj1_sig` of `R_complete`), so the acceptance tests are structural rather than numeric. Distinct from `Construction/Karoubi/Universal.v:416`'s `CauchyComplete := IdempotentsSplit`, which is categorical Cauchy completeness and unrelated; both headers say so. Deferred: the completion as a FUNCTOR and the §IV.3 reflection `Completion ⊣ CMet_Incl` (both machine-checked to elaborate with no further obligation, guarded in the probe file, but left off the library surface since the issue stops at the universal arrow), no metric topology or `Met ⟶ Top`, no short/uniformly-continuous morphism variants, no limits or monoidal structure on `Met`, and no unification with the Lawvere/Cost-enriched reading
- **Instance/Proset/Galois.v**: Galois connections are adjunctions between preorders (Mac Lane §IV.5 Theorem 1) — `GaloisConnection RA RB` (two monotone maps plus the biconditional `f a ⊑ b ↔ a ≤ g b`), the bridge BOTH ways (`GaloisAdjunction`, `GaloisOfAdjunction`), and `galois_round_trip` showing the two maps survive the round trip ON THE NOSE. That round trip is the load-bearing check, not a formality: over a thin target the coherence fields carry nothing, so it is what pins down that the bridge transports the Galois data rather than discarding it. **Thinness is now a usable hypothesis**: `Thin C` was previously available only as the trivial hom-setoid buried inside `Proset` plus an ad-hoc `two_thin`, so it could be neither assumed nor concluded; `proset_thin`, `thin_side_condition` and `proset_side_conditions_vacuous` make the vacuity a REUSABLE LEMMA — in a thin category every one of `Adjunction`'s four naturality fields and `Adjunction_Transform`'s two triangle identities is an equation between parallel morphisms, hence free. Non-vacuity is witnessed concretely by `nat_shift_galois`: on ℕ, truncated SUBTRACTION is left adjoint to ADDITION. NOTE the orientation — "add k ⊣ subtract k" is FALSE (`gal_from` would read `a ≤ b − k → a + k ≤ b`, refuted at `k=5, b=0, a=0` where truncation makes the hypothesis hold and the conclusion not); `lia` rejected it. **Erratum in #380**: `Poset` and `Proset` are the SAME category — `Instance/Poset.v:116-117` is `Definition Poset ... := Proset P`, DISCARDING antisymmetry, so `poset_is_proset` holds by `eq_refl`, anything proved for `Proset` holds for `Poset` by delta-reduction, and antisymmetry is NOT recoverable from the category; a "mutually related ⇒ equal" corollary therefore needs it as a separate hypothesis (`mutual_le_to_eq`).
- **Instance/Pos.v**: `Pos`, the category of posets and monotone maps (Awodey §1.4) — the STRUCTURED-SETS reading, whose OBJECTS are posets, as distinct from `Instance/Poset.v` which turns ONE poset into a thin category. Neither subsumes the other. `Instance/Poset.v` cannot be reused as the donor: its `Poset` (:118-119) is `Definition Poset ... := Proset P`, DISCARDING antisymmetry, so a `Poset` value *is* a `Proset` value and the structure has to be bundled — which `PosetObject` does. It carries a field `Instance/Poset.v` lacks — `pos_le_respects`, the order being compatible with the carrier's own `≈` — which is an object WELL-FORMEDNESS condition, not consumed by any proof below (the file compiles without it); it is kept for the same reason `Instance/CMon.v` carries `cmon_plus_respects`. `pos_antisym` concludes `x ≈ y`, not Leibniz equality — that is what makes this the category of POSETS (the category itself compiles without it), and a disclosed deviation from Awodey's set-level statement. Morphisms are `MonoHom` (a `SetoidMorphism` plus monotonicity), compared pointwise up to `≈`; Awodey's two closure facts (identities monotone, composites monotone) are the discharged obligations of `mono_hom_id`/`mono_hom_compose`. Plus `Pos_Forget : Pos ⟶ Sets`, and the bridge to the other reading — `PosetAsCategory` sends a poset to its thin category and `MonotoneAsFunctor` a monotone map to the induced functor. NOTE #641's "alternative" (realize `Pos` as a full subcategory of `Cat` on "the skeletal thin categories `Poset P`") was not taken, for the reason the issue itself gives — it calls the direct construction the recommended principal artifact. The subcategory is in fact constructible in a few lines (`Construction/Subcategory.v:32`'s `sobj : C → Type` takes any `Type`-valued predicate, needing no equality on `Category` values), and "skeletal thin" does NOT misdescribe its family — skeletality is what antisymmetry buys and strictly excludes preorders. What is genuinely deferred is the AGREEMENT between the two presentations, which needs the carrier setoid and order recovered from a skeletal thin category.

### Applied Programming (Theory/Coq/)
Bridges pure theory with practical Coq programming:
- Applicative functors proven via monoidal functors
- Traversable functors with laws
- Monad transformers with coherence conditions — `MonadTransformer` and `MonadTransformerLaws` are in **Monad/Transformer.v**, NOT under `Theory/Coq/`; they are listed here because they belong to this applied layer thematically

## Critical Design Patterns

### Equivalence Over Equality
Never use `=` for morphisms. Always use `≈`:
```coq
(* WRONG *)
Lemma foo : f ∘ id = f.

(* RIGHT *)
Lemma foo : f ∘ id ≈ f.
```

### Proper Morphisms
All operations must respect equivalence:
```coq
Program Instance Foo_Proper {C : Category} :
  Proper (equiv ==> equiv ==> equiv) (@foo C).
```

### Notation Precedence
- `~>` morphisms (level 90)
- `∘` composition (level 40, left associative)
- `⟶` functors between categories
- `⟹` natural transformations

### Proof Automation
Standard proof pattern:
```coq
Proof.
  intros.
  cat.          (* tries simplification + rewriting *)
  proper.       (* if proving Proper *)
  equivalence.  (* if proving Equivalence *)
Qed.
```

## Common Development Tasks

### Adding a New Category Instance
1. Define objects and morphisms
2. Define equivalence relation on morphisms (setoid)
3. Prove it forms a category (id, compose, laws)
4. Place in Instance/ directory
5. Add to _CoqProject

### Proving Functoriality
1. Define object mapping F_obj
2. Define morphism mapping F_map
3. Prove F_map respects equivalence
4. Prove F preserves id and composition
5. Use `Program Instance` to manage obligations

### Establishing Adjunctions
1. Define functors F : C ⟶ D and G : D ⟶ C
2. Define unit η : Id ⟹ G ○ F
3. Define counit ε : F ○ G ⟹ Id
4. Prove triangle identities
5. Or use hom-set adjunction definition

## References and Learning

When working with specific concepts, reference:
- **nLab**: https://ncatlab.org/nlab/show/[concept_name]
- **README.md**: Contains detailed notation guide (the "Notations" section)
- **docs/AXIOMS.md**: the axiom audit — what each `Axiom`/`Parameter` is, which definitions are certified "Closed under the global context", and which stdlib axioms the concrete instance layers do use.
- **docs/INHABITATION.md**: which headline results carry a concrete in-tree witness of their distinctive premise, and which are proven parametrically as axiom-free conditionals awaiting a model. Consult it before citing a flagship theorem (GAFT, SAFT, the Sheaf theory, `image_mediator_epic`, the spider results, …) as demonstrated over a concrete object — several are conditional-only.
- **Key Files and Concepts** (above): the per-module index of the library's headline developments
- **In-file background essays**: beyond the definitional header most files carry, the flagship concept files across Theory/, Structure/, Construction/, Instance/, Adjunction/, and the Comonad development open with a background essay — the concept's history, purpose, cross-disciplinary use, and in-tree connections, each substantive claim cited to a primary source (nLab, the named papers, textbooks, or package documentation). Read that block first when approaching an unfamiliar file.

## Versioning

Default version: Rocq 9.1 (the `default` package in flake.nix)
Supported: Coq 8.19-8.20, Rocq 9.0-9.1
Equations dependency required for some parts (versions matched to Coq)

## Testing Individual Theorems

To test a specific construction:
```bash
# Extract just the files needed
coqdep -R . Category Theory/MyConstruction.v | grep -v "^#"

# Compile dependencies first
make Theory/Category.vo Theory/Functor.vo

# Then compile your file
coqc -R . Category Theory/MyConstruction.v
```
